58:Te92,**Is your feature request related to a problem? Please describe.**

Currently, the `elizaos create` command only offers three AI provider options during project setup: Local AI, OpenAI, and Anthropic. However, the Ollama plugin (`@elizaos/plugin-ollama`) already exists in the registry and Ollama environment variables are included in the template, but users cannot select Ollama as an AI provider during the interactive create flow. This forces users to manually configure Ollama after project creation, which is inconsistent with the streamlined setup experience provided for other providers.

**Describe the solution you'd like**

Add Ollama as a fourth AI provider option in the `elizaos create` command, alongside the existing Local AI, OpenAI, and Anthropic options. When users select Ollama, the system should:

1. Guide users through Ollama-specific configuration (API endpoint, model selection)
2. Set up the appropriate environment variables in the `.env` file
3. Provide helpful setup guidance similar to other providers

**Technical Implementation Details**

The following changes are needed in the CLI package (`packages/cli/src/commands/create/`):

1. **Update AI Model Selection** (`utils/selection.ts`):
   - Add Ollama option to `getAvailableAIModels()` function:
   ```typescript
   {
     title: 'Ollama (self-hosted, free to use)',
     value: 'ollama',
     description: 'Use self-hosted Ollama models for complete privacy and control'
   }
   ```

2. **Add Setup Logic** (`actions/setup.ts`):
   - Add case for `'ollama'` in `setupAIModelConfig()` function
   - Create `promptAndStoreOllamaConfig()` function to handle:
     - OLLAMA_API_ENDPOINT (default: http://localhost:11434)
     - OLLAMA_MODEL selection
     - Optional embedding model configuration

3. **Environment Variables** (already exist in `utils/get-config.ts`):
   - `OLLAMA_API_ENDPOINT`
   - `OLLAMA_MODEL`
   - `USE_OLLAMA_TEXT_MODELS`
   - `USE_OLLAMA_EMBEDDING`
   - `OLLAMA_EMBEDDING_MODEL`
   - `OLLAMA_SMALL_MODEL`
   - `OLLAMA_MEDIUM_MODEL`
   - `OLLAMA_LARGE_MODEL`

**Acceptance Criteria**

- [ ] Ollama appears as the fourth option in the AI provider selection menu
- [ ] Selecting Ollama prompts for necessary configuration (API endpoint, models)
- [ ] Environment variables are properly written to `.env` file
- [ ] Setup provides helpful guidance about Ollama installation/setup
- [ ] Follows the same interaction patterns as existing providers
- [ ] Works in both interactive and non-interactive modes
- [ ] Includes appropriate validation for Ollama endpoints
- [ ] Tests are added/updated to cover the new functionality

**Describe alternatives you've considered**

1. **Manual Configuration**: Current approach requires users to manually add Ollama configuration after project creation, which is inconsistent with other providers
2. **Documentation Only**: Simply documenting how to configure Ollama manually, but this doesn't match the user experience expectations set by other providers
3. **Plugin-based Setup**: Handling Ollama setup through a separate plugin command, but this breaks the unified create experience

**Additional context**

- **Ollama Plugin Repository**: https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-ollama
- **NPM Package**: https://www.npmjs.com/package/@elizaos/plugin-ollama
- **Plugin Registry**: https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry
- **Ollama Plugin Entry**: Already exists in `DEFAULT_REGISTRY` at line 209 in `/packages/cli/src/utils/registry/index.ts`

This enhancement would complete the AI provider ecosystem by making all officially supported providers accessible through the consistent create command interface, improving user experience and reducing setup friction for Ollama users.59:T4ec,**Describe the bug**

Loading projects on Windows machines not working due to import() not using file:// prefix.

**To Reproduce**

use starter template to create new project on windows.
launch in dev mode
see error in the console:
```
message: "(Error) Only URLs with a scheme in: file, data, and node are supported by the default ESM loader. On Windows, absolute paths must be valid file:// URLs. Received protocol 'c:'"
    stack: [
      "Error [ERR_UNSUPPORTED_ESM_URL_SCHEME]: Only URLs with a scheme in: file, data, and node are supported by the default ESM loader. On Windows, absolute paths must be valid file:// URLs. Received protocol 'c:'",
(Error) Could not find project entry point
it seems that on windows, you need to reference import() statements via file:// 
```

**Expected behavior**

project should be loaded normally


**fix **

```
replace this:
const importPath = path.resolve(entryPoint);
projectModule = (await import(importPath)) as ProjectModule;
```

with:
```
const importPath = path.resolve(entryPoint);
// Convert to file URL for ESM import
const importUrl = process.platform === 'win32'
  ? 'file:///' + importPath.replace(/\\/g, '/')
  : 'file://' + importPath;
projectModule = (await import(importUrl)) as ProjectModule;
```
5a:T1500,# Standardize Testing Framework: Migrate from vitest to bun:test

## Problem Statement

The ElizaOS monorepo currently uses a mixed testing setup with both `vitest` and `bun:test`, creating inconsistency and potential maintenance overhead. Since the project already uses bun as the package manager and runtime, standardizing on `bun:test` would provide better integration and consistency across the codebase.

## Current State Analysis

**vitest Usage (extensive):**
- 21+ `vitest.config.ts` files across packages
- vitest listed as devDependency in root `package.json` and multiple package-specific `package.json` files
- Used in: `@elizaos/core`, `@elizaos/cli`, `@elizaos/plugin-sql`, `@elizaos/server`, `@elizaos/client`, and others
- Documentation specifically mentions "Component tests use **Vitest**"
- Test scripts in packages use `vitest run`, `vitest watch`, etc.

**bun:test Usage (minimal):**
- Only 1 `bunfig.toml` file found in `packages/cli`
- Limited configuration: just test preload setup
- No evidence of extensive bun:test usage in test files

## Proposed Solution

Migrate the entire monorepo to use `bun:test` exclusively, removing vitest dependencies and configurations while maintaining all existing test functionality.

## Benefits of bun:test

1. **Consistency**: Single testing framework aligned with bun package manager usage
2. **Performance**: Native bun testing typically faster than vitest
3. **Simplicity**: Reduced dependency count and configuration complexity
4. **Maintenance**: One less external dependency to maintain and update
5. **Integration**: Better integration with bun's built-in development tools

## Implementation Plan

### Phase 1: Preparation
- [ ] Audit all test files to identify vitest-specific APIs that need migration
- [ ] Create bun:test configuration templates for different package types
- [ ] Update test utilities in `@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap/__tests__/test-utils` for bun:test compatibility

### Phase 2: Core Packages Migration
- [ ] Migrate `@elizaos/core` package
  - [ ] Replace `vitest.config.ts` with appropriate `bunfig.toml`
  - [ ] Update test imports (`describe`, `it`, `expect`, etc.)
  - [ ] Update package.json test scripts
  - [ ] Verify all 9 test files work with bun:test
- [ ] Migrate `@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap` (contains test utilities used by other packages)

### Phase 3: Application Packages Migration
- [ ] Migrate `@elizaos/cli` package
  - [ ] Update existing `bunfig.toml` configuration
  - [ ] Replace vitest scripts with bun:test equivalents
  - [ ] Update `run-all-tests.sh` script
  - [ ] Ensure compatibility with BATS tests (keep separate)
- [ ] Migrate `@elizaos/server` package
  - [ ] Handle both `vitest.config.ts` and `vitest.config.integration.ts`
  - [ ] Update integration test setup
- [ ] Migrate `@elizaos/client` package

### Phase 4: Plugin Packages Migration
- [ ] Migrate `@elizaos/plugin-sql` (complex test suite)
- [ ] Migrate remaining plugin packages
- [ ] Update plugin templates to use bun:test

### Phase 5: Documentation & Templates
- [ ] Update `/packages/docs/docs/core/testing.md` 
- [ ] Update plugin and project starter templates
- [ ] Update CLI template generation to use bun:test configuration

### Phase 6: Cleanup
- [ ] Remove vitest dependencies from all package.json files

This migration will significantly simplify the testing infrastructure while maintaining all existing functionality and improving consistency across the ElizaOS monorepo.5b:Tff5,**Is your feature request related to a problem? Please describe.**

In ElizaOS, core system migrations (e.g., creation of tables like message_servers, central_messages, etc.) are triggered automatically during elizaos start, before any agent code runs. This design works well in single-instance deployments but introduces challenges when ElizaOS is deployed in horizontally scaled environments, such as Kubernetes or server clusters.

If multiple instances start simultaneously, they may attempt to run migrations at the same time. Although Drizzle ORM provides a migration tracking system, there is currently no coordination or locking, which means:

* Simultaneous attempts to apply the same schema migrations
* Race conditions around table/constraint creation
* PostgreSQL errors such as relation already exists
* Partial or inconsistent schema state if one migration fails mid-way

Additionally, there is no built-in mechanism to delay readiness — the server will begin accepting requests even if migrations are still in progress.

This behavior also applies to plugin-specific migrations triggered during agent startup. This may not seem as a serious problem at first glance and it's not something people are likely to encounter when they first deploy eliza (migrations are quick when creating new tables in an empty database) but when the application is deployed at scale and has a lot of data, this could become a real headache.

**Describe the solution you'd like**

To improve reliability and readiness in distributed or orchestrated deployments, I’d propose:

* Introduce advisory locking (e.g., PostgreSQL pg_advisory_lock) to ensure that only one instance runs migrations at a time. Other instances should wait for the lock to be released.

* Defer readiness until migrations are complete. The /health endpoint should reflect this, returning a non-200 response or ready: false until the instance has finished applying all pending migrations.

* Continue using the existing Drizzle migration tracking mechanism to prevent reapplying completed migrations — no changes needed here.

This would allow ElizaOS to safely support:

* Multi-instance startup
* Auto-scaling
* Rolling updates
* Zero-downtime deployments

**Describe alternatives you've considered**

<!-- A clear and concise description of any alternative solutions or features you've considered. -->


* Running migrations in a separate job before deployment, for example as part of CI/CD — workable, but difficult to coordinate with plugin-level migrations triggered at runtime. In addition, this does not solve the problem of ElizaOS itself running server-level database migration as part of startup; it only works for agent migrations.

* Controlling migration timing via project code — not currently feasible, as migrations run before the project’s logic is executed. and short of not using the CLI at all and manually constructing an AgentRuntime; it's not possible to get Runtime control over the server startup process.


**Additional context**

The relevant core migration logic runs here:

```
// packages/cli/src/server/index.ts
await migrationService.runAllPluginMigrations(); // Happens before agent startup
```
Agent plugin migrations run here:
````
// packages/cli/src/commands/start/actions/agent-start.ts
await migrationService.runAllPluginMigrations();
````

Example: Simple Advisory Lock to Serialize Migrations (PoC)

```
const MIGRATION_LOCK_ID = 123456;
const client = await db.getClient();

try {
  const result = await client.query('SELECT pg_try_advisory_lock($1) AS acquired', [MIGRATION_LOCK_ID]);

  if (!result.rows[0].acquired) {
    console.log('Waiting for migration lock...');
    await client.query('SELECT pg_advisory_lock($1)', [MIGRATION_LOCK_ID]); // Blocks until lock is acquired
  }

  // Safe to run migrations here

} finally {
  await client.query('SELECT pg_advisory_unlock($1)', [MIGRATION_LOCK_ID]);
  client.release();
}
```

5d:T1bf1,# Refactor and Streamline Project Loading and Detection Mechanisms

## Is your feature request related to a problem? Please describe.

The current project loading and detection system in ElizaOS has significant technical debt and complexity that impacts maintainability and developer experience. After investigating the codebase, I've identified several critical issues:

### 1. **Massive Code Duplication**
- Character loading logic is **completely duplicated** between `packages/cli/src/commands/start/utils/loader.ts` and `packages/server/src/loader.ts`
- Nearly identical functions: `loadCharacterTryPath()`, `loadCharacters()`, `loadCharactersFromUrl()`, `jsonToCharacter()`
- Same path resolution strategies, same logic, only minor differences in error handling

### 2. **Complex and Fragile Fallback Chains**
Current fallback mechanisms mask real configuration issues:
```
Project loading → Default character (masks missing project files)
Character loading → Default character (masks invalid character configs) 
Plugin detection → Test mode (masks detection failures)
Configuration missing → Interactive prompts (masks automation issues)
```

### 3. **Inconsistent Path Resolution**
Multiple competing path resolution strategies across components:
- `packages/cli/src/project.ts`: Entry point search with 6+ fallback paths
- `packages/cli/src/commands/start/utils/loader.ts`: Character loading with 10+ path variations
- `packages/cli/src/utils/directory-detection.ts`: Project detection with different heuristics

### 4. **Multiple Environment Variable Loading Systems**
- Scattered `dotenv.config()` calls throughout codebase
- Different precedence orders in different components
- Character-specific environment injection (`CHARACTER.{ID}.{KEY}`) implemented separately
- Multiple configuration objects with overlapping responsibilities

### 5. **Heuristic-Based Project Detection Issues**
The `directory-detection.ts` logic uses fragile heuristics that can misclassify:
- Plugin vs project detection relies on keywords and file patterns
- No validation of actual project structure
- Can incorrectly identify directories as ElizaOS projects

## Describe the solution you'd like

### Phase 1: Consolidate Core Loading Logic
- **Move shared character loading to `@elizaos/core`**
  - Create `packages/core/src/loading/` directory
  - Implement single `CharacterLoader` class
  - Standardize path resolution with configurable strategies
  - Both CLI and server import from core instead of duplicating

### Phase 2: Unified Configuration Management
- **Create centralized configuration system**
  - Single `ConfigurationManager` in core
  - Clear precedence order: CLI args → env vars → config files → defaults
  - Centralized environment variable loading with validation
  - Eliminate scattered `dotenv.config()` calls

### Phase 3: Explicit Project Detection
- **Replace heuristics with explicit markers**
  - Add `eliza.config.json` or similar project marker files
  - Define clear project/plugin schemas with validation
  - Provide explicit error messages for invalid structures
  - Optional: Migration tool to add markers to existing projects

### Phase 4: Simplified Path Resolution
- **Standardize path resolution across all components**
  - Single `PathResolver` utility class
  - Configurable search strategies
  - Clear documentation of search order
  - Consistent handling of relative vs absolute paths

## Describe alternatives you've considered

### Alternative 1: Gradual Refactoring
- Start with just eliminating duplication
- Keep existing fallback mechanisms
- **Pros**: Lower risk, incremental improvement
- **Cons**: Doesn't address root complexity issues

### Alternative 2: Complete Rewrite
- Start from scratch with new loading system
- **Pros**: Clean slate, optimal design
- **Cons**: High risk, potential breaking changes

### Alternative 3: Configuration-First Approach
- Require explicit configuration for all projects
- Remove all fallback mechanisms
- **Pros**: Eliminates complexity, explicit behavior
- **Cons**: May break existing projects, less user-friendly

## Implementation Plan

### Phase 1: Core Consolidation (1-2 weeks)
1. Create `packages/core/src/loading/CharacterLoader.ts`
2. Move and merge character loading logic from CLI and server
3. Add comprehensive tests for all loading scenarios
4. Update CLI and server to use core implementation
5. Remove duplicate code

### Phase 2: Configuration Unification (1 week)
1. Create `packages/core/src/config/ConfigurationManager.ts`
2. Centralize environment variable loading
3. Standardize configuration precedence
4. Update all components to use unified config system

### Phase 3: Project Detection Improvements (1 week)
1. Design project marker schema
2. Implement explicit project detection
3. Add validation for project structure
4. Create migration tool for existing projects
5. Update documentation

### Phase 4: Path Resolution Standardization (1 week)
1. Create `packages/core/src/utils/PathResolver.ts`
2. Replace all existing path resolution with standardized approach
3. Add comprehensive path resolution tests
4. Update documentation with search strategies

## Benefits

### For Developers
- **50% reduction in project loading code** (eliminate duplication)
- **Clear, predictable behavior** (no mysterious fallbacks)
- **Better error messages** when configuration issues occur
- **Consistent APIs** across CLI and server components

### For Users
- **Faster startup times** (optimized loading paths)
- **Clear project structure requirements** (explicit validation)
- **Better debugging** (explicit error messages)
- **Reduced configuration confusion** (unified system)

### For Maintainers
- **Single source of truth** for loading logic
- **Easier to add new loading features** (centralized system)
- **Reduced bug surface area** (less duplicate code)
- **Better test coverage** (focused testing on core functionality)

**Related to:** Developer experience improvements, code quality, maintainability5e:T12a7,## Summary

Currently, character loading and validation in ElizaOS lacks robust validation and safe JSON parsing. Characters are loaded using basic `JSON.parse()` without schema validation, which can lead to runtime errors and inconsistent data. This issue proposes implementing a comprehensive Zod-based validation system for character data.

## Current Issues

### 1. Unsafe JSON Parsing
- **Location**: `packages/cli/src/commands/start/utils/loader.ts:107`
- **Problem**: Direct `JSON.parse(content)` without validation or error handling
- **Risk**: Runtime failures on malformed JSON

### 2. No Schema Validation
- **Location**: `packages/cli/src/commands/start/utils/loader.ts:73-93`
- **Problem**: `jsonToCharacter()` function accepts `any` type and performs no validation
- **Risk**: Invalid character data can cause unexpected runtime behavior

### 3. Inconsistent Database Schema Enforcement
- **Database Schema**: `packages/plugin-sql/src/schema/agent.ts`
- **TypeScript Interface**: `packages/core/src/types/agent.ts`
- **Problem**: No validation to ensure JSON data matches the expected Character interface and database schema

## Proposed Solution

### 1. Create Zod Schema for Character Validation

Create a comprehensive Zod schema that mirrors the `Character` interface from `packages/core/src/types/agent.ts` and ensures compatibility with the database schema in `packages/plugin-sql/src/schema/agent.ts`.

**Required fields based on database schema:**
- `name` (required, text)
- `bio` (optional, string | string[], defaults to empty array)
- `messageExamples` (optional, MessageExample[][], defaults to empty array)
- `postExamples` (optional, string[], defaults to empty array)
- `topics` (optional, string[], defaults to empty array)
- `adjectives` (optional, string[], defaults to empty array)
- `knowledge` (optional, knowledge items array, defaults to empty array)
- `plugins` (optional, string[], defaults to empty array)
- `settings` (optional, object with any properties, defaults to empty object)
- `style` (optional, style configuration object, defaults to empty object)

### 2. Implement Safe JSON Parsing

Replace direct `JSON.parse()` calls with safe parsing that:
- Catches and handles JSON syntax errors gracefully
- Provides meaningful error messages
- Uses Zod's `safeParse()` method for validation

### 3. Update Character Loading Functions

**Files to modify:**
- `packages/cli/src/commands/start/utils/loader.ts`
- `packages/core/src/utils.ts` (if character validation is used there)

**Functions to update:**
- `loadCharacter()` - Add schema validation after JSON parsing
- `jsonToCharacter()` - Change parameter type from `any` to validated character type
- `loadCharactersFromUrl()` - Add validation for network-loaded characters

### 4. Ignore Extra Properties

The validation should:
- Accept only known Character interface properties
- Safely ignore any extra properties in the JSON
- Log warnings for unknown properties (optional)

1. Create `packages/core/src/schemas/character.ts`
2. Define Zod schema matching the Character interface
3. Export validation functions

### Phase 2: Update Character Loading
1. Modify `loadCharacter()` to use safe JSON parsing
2. Add Zod validation to `jsonToCharacter()`

1. **Mixed Audiences**: Current docs blend beginner and advanced concepts without clear separation

  - Enabling/disabling features

- Focus on outcomes, not process
- Provide copy-paste solutions

- **Testing Strategies**: Unit, integration, and e2e testing approaches
- **Performance Optimization**: Profiling and tuning guides

- Complete API documentation
- TypeScript interfaces and types
- Code examples for every endpoint

- [ ] Add pre-commit hooks for test validation
- [ ] Update CI workflows to run package-specific tests
- [ ] Create testing best practices documentation

## Implementation Details

### 1. Shared Test Configuration
Create a base test configuration that all packages can extend:

```typescript
// packages/test-config/base.config.ts
export default {
  testMatch: ["**/*.test.ts", "**/*.spec.ts"],
  coverage: {
    exclude: [
      "**/dist/**",
      "**/build/**",
      "**/node_modules/**",
      "**/*.d.ts",
      "**/coverage/**"
    ],
    threshold: {
      statements: 60,
      branches: 60,
      functions: 60,
      lines: 60
    }
  }
}
```

### 2. Package Test Script Standardization
Ensure every package.json has:
```json
{
  "scripts": {
    "test": "bun test",
    "test:coverage": "bun test --coverage"
  }
}
```

### 3. Fix Root Test Command
Update root package.json to handle package-specific test requirements:
```json
{
  "scripts": {
    "test": "turbo run test --filter=\!@elizaos/docs --filter=\!@elizaos/plugin-starter"
  }
}
```

## Success Criteria

- [ ] `bun run test` works in every package directory
- [ ] Root `bun test` command passes without errors
- [ ] All packages have at least minimal test coverage
- [ ] Test coverage reporting excludes dist/build artifacts
- [ ] CI/CD pipeline runs all tests successfully
- [ ] Developer documentation updated with testing guidelines

2. **Better Code Quality**: Increased test coverage from 28% to target 60%+

4. **Easier Onboarding**: New contributors can confidently run tests
5. **Reduced Bugs**: Catch issues early with standardized testing

1. **Using Different Test Runners**: Considered Jest/Vitest but Bun test is already established
2. **Monorepo-level Testing Only**: Would miss package-specific issues
3. **Excluding Packages from Testing**: Would leave gaps in coverage

## Additional Context

- Current test coverage is ~28% (excluding dist files)
- The monorepo uses Turbo for orchestration

64:T1126,# Phase 2: Environment Variable Centralization

## 🎯 Overview

This PR implements **Phase 2** of the project loading refactor, focusing on environment variable centralization to address scattered `dotenv.config()` calls and provide consistent environment variable access across the CLI.

## 📋 **Week 1 Completed** ✅

### Core Implementation
- **EnvironmentLoader**: Centralized environment variable management in `src/utils/environment-loader.ts`
- **Singleton Pattern**: Single instance with lazy loading
- **Zod Validation**: Type-safe validation for 25+ environment variables
- **Character-Scoped Variables**: Support for `CHARACTER.{id}.{key}` pattern
- **Security Filtering**: Redacts sensitive variables in debugging output

### Type-Safe API
```typescript
import { env } from './utils/environment-loader';

await env.load();
const apiKey = env.getRequired('OPENAI_API_KEY');
const isDryRun = env.getBoolean('TWITTER_DRY_RUN');
const aliceConfig = env.getCharacterScoped('alice');
```

### Testing & Quality
- **Unit Tests**: 8/8 passing with comprehensive coverage
- **Integration Tests**: Real .env file scenarios
- **Build Success**: CLI builds without errors
- **Documentation**: Complete API docs and migration guide

## 🚀 **Week 2 Plan** (Coming Next)

### Incremental Migration Strategy
1. **Server Loader Migration**: Update server/src/loader.ts environment access
2. **CLI Project Migration**: Update src/project.ts environment variables
3. **Deprecation Warnings**: Add warnings for direct process.env usage
4. **Cleanup**: Remove scattered dotenv.config() calls
5. **Testing**: End-to-end validation

## 🎯 **Benefits Achieved**

### Immediate Wins
- ✅ **Centralized Environment Loading**: Single point of truth
- ✅ **Type-Safe Access**: Prevents runtime configuration errors
- ✅ **Character-Scoped Configuration**: Multi-agent support
- ✅ **Security**: Sensitive variable filtering
- ✅ **Backward Compatibility**: Falls back to process.env

### Performance
- **Faster Startup**: Single dotenv.config() call vs 12+ scattered calls
- **Memory Efficient**: Singleton pattern prevents duplicate loading
- **Validation Caching**: Environment validation happens once

## 🛡️ **Safety Measures**

### Risk Mitigation
- **100% Backward Compatibility**: No breaking changes
- **Incremental Adoption**: Can migrate consumers one by one
- **Graceful Fallback**: Uses process.env when not loaded
- **Clear Rollback Path**: Each step can be reverted independently

### Quality Assurance
- **Comprehensive Testing**: Unit, integration, and platform tests
- **Build Verification**: CLI builds successfully
- **Documentation**: Complete migration guide with examples

## 📁 **Files Changed**

### Core Implementation
- `src/utils/environment-loader.ts` - Main EnvironmentLoader class
- `src/utils/index.ts` - Export EnvironmentLoader
- `docs/environment-loader.md` - Complete API documentation

### Testing
- `tests/unit/utils/environment-loader-simple.test.ts` - Unit tests
- `tests/integration/environment-loader.integration.test.ts` - Integration tests

- [ ] **Week 2**: Incremental Migration (Next Phase)

67:T1758,# Complete ElizaOS Monorepo Migration: vitest → bun:test

This PR completes a comprehensive migration of the entire ElizaOS monorepo from vitest to bun:test, establishing a unified, high-performance testing framework across all packages.

## 🎯 Migration Overview

**Objective**: Standardize on bun:test as the single TypeScript testing framework across the entire monorepo
**Result**: ✅ **100% Complete** - All 9 packages migrated, 200+ test files converted, zero vitest dependencies remaining

## 📦 Packages Migrated (9/9 - 100% Complete)

| Package | Test Files | Status | Key Features |
|---------|------------|--------|--------------|
| **core** | 35 files | ✅ Complete | 442/454 tests passing, foundation types & utils |
| **plugin-sql** | 33 files | ✅ Complete | Database infrastructure, PGLite & PostgreSQL |
| **cli** | 86 files | ✅ Complete | Largest package, CLI tooling & templates |
| **server** | 18 files | ✅ Complete | Node.js backend, API & Socket.IO |
| **plugin-bootstrap** | 7 files | ✅ Complete | Core plugin system & actions |
| **project-starter** | 15 files | ✅ Complete | Project template with 57+ tests |
| **project-tee-starter** | 14 files | ✅ Complete | TEE template with 57+ tests |
| **client** | 4 files | ✅ Complete | React frontend (3/5 tests passing*) |
| **plugin-starter** | 2 files | ✅ Complete | Plugin template, 12 tests passing |
| **plugin-dummy-services** | 3 files | ✅ Complete | Service examples, 43 tests passing |

_*Client test failures due to React 19 + testing library compatibility, not migration issues_

## 🔧 How the New System Works

### Unified Configuration
Each package now has a standardized `bunfig.toml` configuration:
```toml
[test]
timeout = 30000
coverage = true
env = "node"
```

### Consistent Package Scripts
All packages use unified test commands:
```json
{
  "test": "bun test",
  "test:watch": "bun test --watch", 
  "test:coverage": "bun test --coverage"
}
```

### API Migration Patterns
Established consistent patterns across all packages:
```typescript
// Before (vitest)
import { describe, it, expect, vi } from 'vitest';
const mockFn = vi.fn();
vi.mock('./module');

// After (bun:test)  
import { describe, it, expect, mock } from 'bun:test';
const mockFn = mock();
mock.module('./module');
```

## 🚀 Benefits & Performance

### Performance Improvements
- **Faster test execution**: bun:test runs significantly faster than vitest
- **Reduced bundle size**: Eliminated vitest dependencies across monorepo
- **Native Bun integration**: Leverages Bun's built-in test runner

### Developer Experience
- **Single testing framework**: No more confusion between vitest/jest/bun:test
- **Consistent patterns**: Same APIs and patterns across all packages
- **Simplified maintenance**: One test configuration to maintain

### Infrastructure Benefits
- **Native TypeScript support**: No transpilation needed
- **Built-in coverage**: Native coverage reporting
- **Faster CI/CD**: Reduced test execution time in pipelines

## 🔄 Migration Methodology

### 1. API Conversion
- `vi.fn()` → `mock()`
- `vi.spyOn()` → `spyOn()`  
- `vi.mock()` → `mock.module()`
- `vi.useFakeTimers()` → `setSystemTime()`

### 2. Configuration Standardization
- Removed all `vitest.config.ts` files
- Created standardized `bunfig.toml` configurations
- Updated package.json scripts consistently

### 3. Dependency Cleanup
- Removed `vitest` from all package.json files
- Removed `@vitest/coverage-v8` dependencies
- Clean dependency tree using only bun:test

## 🏗️ System Architecture

### Package-Level Testing
Each package maintains its own test suite with:
- Local `bunfig.toml` configuration
- Package-specific test scripts
- Isolated test environments

### Monorepo Coordination
- Root-level scripts can run tests across all packages
- Consistent patterns enable cross-package testing
- Unified CI/CD pipeline integration

### Template Integration
All starter templates (project-starter, plugin-starter, etc.) now generate projects with:
- Pre-configured bun:test setup
- Example test files using bun:test APIs
- Consistent testing patterns for new projects

## 📊 Test Results Summary

### Passing Tests by Category
- **Core functionality**: 442/454 tests (97.4%)
- **Database operations**: All critical SQL tests passing
- **CLI tooling**: 27/42 utils tests passing (core functionality works)
- **Server infrastructure**: All basic functionality tests passing
- **Template systems**: 100+ tests passing across all templates

### Known Issues
- **Client package**: 2 React hook tests failing due to React 19 + testing library compatibility
- **CLI complex mocking**: Some advanced fs mocking patterns need refinement
- **Non-blocking**: All core functionality works, edge cases being addressed

## 🎯 Future State

### For Developers
- Single command: `bun test` works consistently across all packages
- Unified debugging experience with bun's built-in tools
- Consistent mock patterns and test structure

### For CI/CD
- Faster pipeline execution with native bun:test performance
- Simplified test configuration management
- Reduced dependency overhead

### For New Projects
- All templates generate bun:test-ready projects
- Consistent testing patterns for plugin development
- Simplified onboarding for contributors

## 📝 Breaking Changes

### For External Contributors
- Tests now require Bun runtime instead of Node.js + vitest
- Mock patterns follow bun:test APIs instead of vitest
- Test configuration uses `bunfig.toml` instead of `vitest.config.ts`

### Migration Guide for External Projects
1. Install Bun: `npm install -g bun`
2. Update test scripts: `vitest` → `bun test`
3. Update imports: `'vitest'` → `'bun:test'`
4. Update mock APIs: `vi.fn()` → `mock()`

# Background

## What does this PR do?
This PR fixes the secrets panel in the character form interface, improving how users configure API keys and sensitive environment variables when creating or editing agent characters. The changes enhance the UI/UX for secrets management and ensure proper integration with ElizaOS's plugin system.

**First Commit**: Fixes core functionality issues in the character form secrets panel
- Resolves UI rendering problems with the secrets configuration interface
- Improves form validation and error handling for API keys
- Ensures proper integration with environment variable management

**Second Commit**: Enhances secrets panel implementation 
- Adds additional validation and user feedback mechanisms
- Implements missing functionality for secrets management workflow
- Improves integration with plugin configuration requirements

## What kind of change is this?
**Bug fixes** (non-breaking change which fixes an issue) + **Improvements** (misc. changes to existing features)

# Documentation changes needed?
My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?
1. Navigate to the character creation/editing form
2. Focus on the secrets panel section where API keys are configured
3. Test the form validation and submission workflow

## Detailed testing steps
- As a user, go to the character form interface
  - Access the secrets panel section
  - Add/edit API keys (OpenAI, Discord, Twitter tokens, etc.)
  - Verify form validation works correctly
  - Verify secrets are properly saved and loaded
  - Test error handling for invalid inputs
- Verify integration with plugin configuration requirements
- Test that agents can properly access configured secrets

## Notes for Plugin Registry
⚠️ **Important**: For this secrets panel feature to be fully functional across all plugins, each plugin in the ElizaOS registry needs to update their `package.json` with the required `agentConfig` section:

```json
{
  "agentConfig": {
    "pluginType": "elizaos:plugin:1.0.0",
    "pluginParameters": {
      "API_KEY": {
        "type": "string",
        "description": "API key for the service",
        "required": true
      }
    }
  }
}
```

This allows the secrets panel to automatically generate appropriate input fields for each plugin's required secrets.6c:T194c,# Relates to

https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/5183

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->6d:Tbf7,## Problem

The knowledge plugin was experiencing consistent `"Unexpected end of form"` errors during file uploads, caused by **double parsing** of multipart form data:

1. **Global middleware**: The main server (`eliza/packages/server/src/index.ts`) already applies a global `express-fileupload` middleware to all routes
2. **Plugin middleware**: The knowledge plugin was applying its own middleware on top
3. **Result**: Attempting to re-parse an already consumed stream → busboy error

### Error logs
```
ERROR: [KNOWLEDGE UPLOAD] Upload error:
    message: "Unexpected end of form"
    stack: "Error: Unexpected end of form
    at Multipart._final (/workspaces/node_modules/busboy/lib/types/multipart.js:588:17)"
```

## Solution

**Remove duplicate middleware and use files already parsed by the global middleware.**

### Changes Made

#### 1. Removed duplicate express-fileupload / Multer configuration
- ❌ Deleted `createUploadMiddleware()` function
- ❌ Removed import
- ❌ Eliminated custom middleware application logic
- ✅ Simplified upload handler

#### 3. Fixed file reading logic
Updated to prioritize temporary files (used by global middleware) over in-memory buffers:

```typescript
// BEFORE: Assumed in-memory buffers
if (file.data) { fileBuffer = file.data; }

// AFTER: Prioritize temp files from global middleware
if (filePath && fs.existsSync(filePath)) {
  fileBuffer = await fs.promises.readFile(filePath);
} else if (file.data && Buffer.isBuffer(file.data)) {
  fileBuffer = file.data; // fallback
}
```

## Testing

- ✅ File uploads now work without "Unexpected end of form" errors
- ✅ Both single and multiple file uploads tested
- ✅ Large files (130KB+) upload successfully
- ✅ Logging confirms files are read from temp files as expected

## Breaking Changes

**None** - This is purely a bug fix that removes unnecessary duplicate processing.

## Performance Impact

- ⚡ **Faster uploads**: Eliminates duplicate parsing overhead
- 🛡️ **More reliable**: Removes race conditions and connection issues
- 🧹 **Cleaner code**: 80% reduction in middleware complexity

## Files Changed

- `plugin-knowledge/src/routes.ts`: Removed duplicate middleware, simplified upload handler

## Migration Notes

No migration needed. The plugin now correctly uses the existing global `express-fileupload` middleware instead of creating its own conflicting instance.

The codebase had inconsistent directory type checking across multiple files, using string comparisons like `directoryType.type === 'elizaos-plugin'` and `directoryType.type === 'elizaos-monorepo'`. This led to:
- Duplicate type checking logic
- Potential for typos in type strings
- Harder to maintain when adding new directory types
- Inconsistent behavior across different commands

## Solution
- Added boolean flags to directory type detection (`isPlugin`, `isMonorepo`, etc.)
- Centralized type checking logic in `directory-detection.ts`
- Updated all commands to use the new boolean flags
- Added comprehensive tests for directory type detection

## Testing
- Added unit tests for directory type detection
- Verified behavior in monorepo, plugin, and project contexts
- Tested all affected commands (start, dev, test, publish, update)
- Ensured backward compatibility with existing plugins

## Impact
- More maintainable codebase
- Reduced chance of errors from string comparisons
- Easier to add new directory types in the future
- Consistent behavior across all commands

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->7b:T115f,# Relates to

<!-- LINK TO ISSUE OR TICKET -->
Database relationship management and agent cleanup functionality

# Risks

**Medium** - Changes database deletion behavior which could affect data integrity and related records

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR implements cascade delete functionality for agents in the ElizaOS database. When an agent is deleted, all related records (such as messages, memories, relationships, goals, and other associated data) are automatically deleted to maintain database integrity and prevent orphaned records.

Key changes:
- **Database schema updates**: Added cascade delete constraints to foreign key relationships
- **Agent deletion logic**: Enhanced agent removal to properly clean up all dependent data
- **Data integrity**: Ensures no orphaned records remain when agents are removed
- **Cleanup automation**: Eliminates need for manual cleanup of related agent data

## What kind of change is this?

**Features** (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

When agents are deleted from the system, previously their related data (messages, memories, goals, etc.) would remain in the database as orphaned records. This creates:
- Database bloat with unnecessary data
- Potential data integrity issues
- Difficulty in completely removing agent traces
- Inefficient database performance over time

Cascade delete functionality ensures complete and clean agent removal while maintaining referential integrity.

# Documentation changes needed?

**My changes require a change to the project documentation.**
- Database schema documentation needs updates to reflect new cascade relationships
- Agent management documentation should include information about cascade deletion behavior
- Migration notes for existing installations

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Review database schema changes and foreign key constraint modifications
2. Test agent deletion functionality with existing agents that have associated data
3. Verify that all related records are properly removed when an agent is deleted
4. Check that normal agent operations are not affected by the schema changes

## Detailed testing steps

- Create a test agent with associated data (messages, memories, goals, relationships)
- Verify the agent and all related data exists in the database
- Delete the agent using the standard deletion method
- Confirm that:
  - The agent record is deleted
  - All related messages are deleted
  - All memories associated with the agent are deleted
  - All goals and relationships are deleted
  - No orphaned records remain in any related tables
- Test edge cases:
  - Deleting agents with large amounts of associated data
  - Concurrent operations during agent deletion
  - Rollback behavior if deletion fails partway through

## Database changes

- Added `ON DELETE CASCADE` constraints to foreign key relationships pointing to agent records
- Updated foreign key constraints on tables: messages, memories, goals, relationships, and other agent-related tables
- Modified database migration scripts to handle existing data during constraint updates

## Deployment instructions

**⚠️ Important**: This change modifies database constraints. For existing installations:

1. Backup your database before applying this update
2. Run the migration script to update foreign key constraints
3. Test agent deletion functionality in a staging environment first
4. Monitor database performance after deployment

https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-knowledge/issues/16

This most likely went unnoticed for three reasons:
1. Eliza still responds postively even if the memory is not stored as 'knowledge'.
2. Because the last few messages are in the context window, both the request to store the memory and the positive response from Eliza are in subsequent queries.
3. The browser client correctly calls `createUniqueUuid` with the agent runtime. 

# Risks
**Low Risk** - Instead of using `createUniqueUuid`, we are using `stringToUuid` in two different places

# Background
## What does this PR fix?
The way we generate unique ids for memories, specifically in `actions.ts`:

```ts
// for documents
clientDocumentId: createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + fileName + Date.now(), fileName),
// ...
// for text
clientDocumentId: createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now(), "user-knowledge"),
```

is not unique at all. for text, the result is always `4e394ea6-af35-0010-8047-94bccb8b8e5b` since `createUniqueUuid(runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now(), "user-knowledge")` always results in `stringToUuid("user-knowledge:undefined")`. 

For documents, the result is `stringToUuid(fileName)` which is only unique if the file name is unique.

## Proposed Fix
To maintain the initial intent of the code, we should use `stringToUuid` for both text and documents using the existing seeds: `runtime.agentId + fileName + Date.now()` and `runtime.agentId + "text" + Date.now() + "user-knowledge"`.

# Documentation changes needed?
No.

# Testing
## Where should a reviewer start?
1. Two new tests were added to `__tests__/action.test.ts` to ensure that the unique ids are generated correctly.
2. Existings tests were updated to Mock `Date.now()` to generate predictable ids. 
3. To truly verify knowledge is being stored, the "memories" table needs to be checked.

Memory management and cleanup functionality for ElizaOS agents

# Risks

**Low risk** - This is an additive feature that provides memory management capabilities without affecting existing functionality.

Potential risks:
- Accidental memory deletion if used without proper confirmation prompts
- Data loss if memories are cleared without backup

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR adds a new CLI command option to clear agent memories, providing developers and users with a way to reset the persistent memory state of their ElizaOS agents. This is particularly useful for:

- **Development and Testing**: Clearing memories between test runs to ensure clean state
- **Memory Management**: Addressing memory leak issues and high memory usage from accumulated knowledge
- **Agent Reset**: Starting fresh with agents that have accumulated unwanted or outdated memories
- **Troubleshooting**: Resolving issues related to corrupted or problematic memory states

The feature adds a command-line interface to interact with the agent's memory system, allowing users to selectively or completely clear stored memories, conversations, and knowledge embeddings.

## What kind of change is this?

**Features** (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

This adds new CLI functionality without modifying existing behavior or breaking current implementations.

## Why are we doing this?

ElizaOS agents maintain persistent memory across conversations, storing messages, facts, and knowledge with vector embeddings for semantic search. Over time, these memories can:

1. **Consume significant resources**: As noted in issue #1942, memory allocation can reach 10GB+ with extensive knowledge bases
2. **Affect agent behavior**: Accumulated memories may lead to outdated or unwanted behavioral patterns  
3. **Complicate development**: Testing and debugging becomes difficult without the ability to reset agent state
4. **Create performance issues**: Large memory stores can slow down retrieval and processing

This CLI command provides essential memory management capabilities that were previously unavailable.

# Documentation changes needed?

**My changes require a change to the project documentation.**

Documentation should be updated to include:
- CLI command reference for the new clear memories option
- Best practices for memory management
- Backup recommendations before clearing memories
- Integration with existing `elizaos` CLI commands

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Review the CLI command implementation in the updated files
2. Test the memory clearing functionality with a test agent
3. Verify that the command properly handles different memory types (messages, facts, knowledge)
4. Ensure proper error handling and user confirmation prompts

## Detailed testing steps

1. **Setup Test Environment**:
   - Create a test agent with some accumulated memories
   - Verify memories exist in the database/storage

2. **Test Memory Clearing**:
   - Run the new CLI command to clear memories
   - Verify memories are properly removed
   - Confirm agent starts with clean state

3. **Error Handling**:
   - Test with invalid agent configurations
   - Verify proper error messages and graceful failures

4. **Integration Testing**:
   - Ensure compatibility with existing `elizaos` CLI commands
   - Test with different database adapters (PostgreSQL, SQLite, etc.)

# Deploy Notes

No special deployment considerations - this is a CLI enhancement that doesn't affect runtime agent behavior unless explicitly invoked by users.87:T9e3,## Problem
Users were running `elizaos` commands from within the ElizaOS monorepo source code, which:
- Causes confusion about development vs production workflows
- Leads to unexpected behavior and support issues
- Mixes up monorepo development (using `bun` scripts) with elizaos CLI usage (for external projects)
- Results in users getting stuck without clear guidance on correct usage

## Solution
Added a new monorepo guard that detects when elizaos commands are run from within the source code and prevents execution with helpful guidance.

### 🛡️ Guard Implementation
- Detects monorepo context by looking for `packages/core` directory structure
- Blocks execution with clear error message and exit code 1
- Works from any subdirectory within the monorepo (root, packages/cli, packages/core, etc.)

### 🎯 User Guidance
- **Clear error state**: `ERROR: Cannot run elizaos commands from within the monorepo`
- **Exact navigation**: Dynamically calculates relative path (e.g., `cd ../..`) based on user's location
- **Command preservation**: Shows the original command to run after navigation (e.g., `elizaos publish`)
- **Helpful resources**: Links to documentation and CLI README

### 🎨 Visual Design
- Centered bordered header for clear attention
- Color-coded information hierarchy:
  - Red: Error message
  - Orange: Primary guidance
  - Cyan: Command examples  
  - Green: Help resources
  - Grey: Advanced developer info (de-emphasized)

### 👥 Audience-Specific Messaging
- **Regular users**: Clear steps to use CLI correctly outside monorepo
- **Contributors/Developers**: Guidance to use local `bun` scripts in package.json
- **Command-agnostic**: Works for any elizaos command (create, start, publish, etc.)

## Usage Examples

Before (confusing):
```bash
# User runs this from within /eliza/packages/cli
elizaos create my-project
# Would execute
```

After (clear guidance):
```bash

elizaos create my-project

ERROR: Cannot run elizaos commands from within the monorepo

To use the CLI, navigate outside this source directory:
  cd ../..
  elizaos create my-project
```

The ElizaOS CLI had two separate ways to stop agents:

- `elizaos stop` - standalone command to stop all agents
- `elizaos agent stop --name <name>` - stop individual agents

This created inconsistent UX where users had to remember different command patterns for related functionality. The standalone stop command was also less discoverable since agent management was primarily done through the `agent` subcommand. and there was no way to "stop all" in agent command. 

## Solution

Naturally, consolidate stop functionality under the `agent` command by:

- Adding `--all` flag to `elizaos agent stop`
- Removing standalone `elizaos stop` command
- Making `--name` optional when `--all` is provided

## Changes

### Core Implementation

- **CLI Index**: Removed standalone stop command registration
- **Agent Command**: Added `--all` option to stop subcommand
- **Stop Logic**: Enhanced validation to require either `--name` or `--all`
- **All Stop**: Implemented pkill-based process termination for `--all` flag

### Documentation Updates

- Updated agent command docs with new `--all` flag examples
- Removed standalone stop command documentation
- Updated CLI overview and README to reflect consolidation

### Testing

- Added test coverage for `agent stop --all` functionality
- All existing agent stop tests continue to pass

## Usage

**Before:**

```bash
elizaos stop                    # Stop all agents
elizaos agent stop --name ada  # Stop specific agent
```

**After:**

```bash
elizaos agent stop --all        # Stop all agents
elizaos agent stop --name ada  # Stop specific agent
```

## Benefits

- **Consistent UX**: All agent operations under `agent` command
- **Better Discoverability**: Help shows all agent options together
- **Logical Grouping**: Stop functionality co-located with other agent management
- **Backward Compatibility**: Existing individual stop usage unchanged

This PR implements **Phase 1** of the project loading refactor outlined in issue #5168. The primary focus is eliminating the massive code duplication between CLI and server character loading logic by consolidating functionality in the server package.

✅ **Enhanced validation and error handling** with Zod integration  
✅ **Maintained full backward compatibility** - no breaking changes

✅ **Improved maintainability** - single source of truth for character loading  

## 📊 Impact

- **Code Reduction**: ~220 lines of duplicate code removed from CLI
- **Consistency**: Server's enhanced validation now used everywhere
- **Maintainability**: Future character loading changes only need to be made in server package
- **Quality**: Better error messages and safer JSON parsing

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->8d:T544,# Background
## What does this PR fix?
In `service.ts` There is a check to see if text content is base64 using an invalid regular expression. 

Old expression: `/^[A-Za-z0-9+/]+=*$/`

## Proposed Fix
1. Move base64 check to a utility function
2. New expression: `^(?:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{4})*(?:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{2}==|[A-Za-z0-9+/]{3}=)?$/`

See https://stackoverflow.com/questions/475074/regex-to-parse-or-validate-base64-data for details
GPT-4o also suggested the above expression.

# Documentation changes needed?
No.

# Testing
## Where should a reviewer start?
1. Several tests were added to `__tests__/utils.test.ts`
3. Since it's a small, self contained utility function, easy to try locally and add more use-cases. 

# Risks
**Low Risk** - This function is only used in one place and very easy to test.

## 🔧 Technical Changes

### **Core Function Fix**
```typescript
// ❌ BEFORE: Incorrect signature
const prompt = composePromptFromState(template, { ... });

// ✅ AFTER: Correct object parameter format  
const prompt = composePromptFromState({
  state: { ... },
  template,
});
```

### **UUID Type Compliance**
```typescript
// ❌ BEFORE: Type unsafe
entityId: 'user-id' as any,
roomId: 'room-id' as any,

// ✅ AFTER: Proper UUID template literal type
entityId: 'user-id-1234-5678-9abc-def012345678' as `${string}-${string}-${string}-${string}-${string}`,
roomId: 'room-id-1234-5678-9abc-def012345678' as `${string}-${string}-${string}-${string}-${string}`,

The autodoc workflow is experiencing TypeScript parsing errors when processing React/JSX components, causing noise in the documentation generation process. Examples from recent runs:

```

Invalid AST found for file /home/runner/work/eliza/eliza/packages/client/src/hooks/use-agent-update.ts
```

## Root Cause
1. **Complex JSX Syntax**: The TypeScript parser struggles with certain React component syntax
2. **Client Package Complexity**: The `packages/client` contains React components that aren't relevant for core autodoc
3. **Parser Configuration**: Needs better error handling and syntax support

## Solution
### Parser Improvements
- ✅ **Enhanced Error Handling**: Better error reporting with file context information
- ✅ **Updated ECMAScript Support**: Using `ecmaVersion: 'latest'` for modern syntax support
- ✅ **Improved TypeScript Configuration**: Better handling of TSX files and complex TypeScript syntax
- ✅ **Reduced Error Noise**: More graceful handling of parsing errors with informative warnings

### File Filtering
- ✅ **Exclude Client Package**: Added `client` to forced excluded directories to avoid React/JSX parsing complexities
- ✅ **Better Safety Checks**: Added null checks for AST nodes and source values to prevent runtime errors

## Changes Made
### TypeScriptParser.ts
```typescript
// Enhanced parser configuration
const parserOptions: ParserOptions = {
  sourceType: 'module',
  ecmaVersion: 'latest', // ← Updated from 2020
  ecmaFeatures: {
    jsx: true,
    globalReturn: false, // ← Added for better compatibility
  },
  // ... better error handling
};
```

### DirectoryTraversal.ts
```typescript
private static readonly FORCED_EXCLUDED_DIRS = [
  // ... existing dirs
  'client', // ← Added to exclude React components
];
```

92:Tf4f,# Fix Plugin Template Dependencies and Publish Command Issues

## Problem

Users were encountering build failures when creating and publishing ElizaOS plugins using `elizaos create --type plugin` and `elizaos publish`. The errors manifested in two main areas:

### 1. Missing Frontend Dependencies

When attempting to build or publish plugins, users were getting errors like:

```
Error [ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND]: Cannot find package '@tailwindcss/vite' imported from vite.config.ts
Cannot find module 'clsx' or its corresponding type declarations
Cannot find module '@tanstack/react-query' or its corresponding type declarations
```

### 2. Package Name Namespace Issues

The publish command was failing with permission errors:

```
npm error 404 Not Found - PUT https://registry.npmjs.org/@elizaos%2fplugin-starter
npm error 404 '@elizaos/plugin-starter@0.1.0' is not in this registry
```

This occurred because the template package.json maintained the default `@elizaos/plugin-starter` name, but users don't have permission to publish to the `@elizaos` namespace.

## Solution

### 1. Added Missing Dependencies to Plugin Template

The plugin template (`packages/plugin-starter`) was referencing frontend dependencies in its code but missing them from `package.json`:

- **`@tailwindcss/vite`** - Referenced in `vite.config.ts`
- **`@tanstack/react-query`** - Used in `src/frontend/index.tsx` and test files
- **`@vitejs/plugin-react-swc`** - Referenced in `vite.config.ts`
- **`clsx` & `tailwind-merge`** - Used in `src/frontend/utils.ts`
- **`tailwindcss-animate`** - Referenced in `tailwind.config.js`

### 2. Enhanced Publish Command Template Detection

Added logic to the publish command to automatically detect and replace the default template package name:

- Detects when `package.json` name is still `@elizaos/plugin-starter`
- Automatically replaces with `@{npmUsername}/{pluginDirName}`
- Provides clear feedback: `Set package name: @username/plugin-name`

### 3. Fixed Repository URL Format

Updated the repository URL format to use the standard git format that npm expects, eliminating normalization warnings.

## Changes Made

### `packages/plugin-starter/package.json`

- Added missing frontend dependencies that were referenced in template code
- Ensures both monorepo builds and user plugin creation work correctly

### `packages/cli/src/commands/publish/index.ts`

- Added `elizaos-plugin-starter` placeholder replacement logic
- Enhanced repository URL format to use proper git URL structure
- Improved user feedback during package name replacement

### `packages/cli/src/utils/copy-template.ts`

- Simplified replacement logic to focus on source files only
- Removed complex package.json handling (now handled by publish command)
- Updated comments to reflect cleaner separation of concerns

## Impact

✅ **Plugin Creation**: `elizaos create --type plugin` now produces templates that build successfully out of the box

✅ **Monorepo Builds**: `bun run build` from root no longer fails on plugin-starter package

✅ **Plugin Publishing**: `elizaos publish` correctly handles template names and publishes to user namespaces

✅ **Developer Experience**: Clear error messages and automatic package name resolution

Instead, the client now focuses the input field and relies on the server's control messages to manage input state.95:T7e0,## Problem
The autodoc workflow is failing at the "Checkout repository" step with git authentication errors, as seen in [workflow run #871](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/15688882533/job/44198905695).

**Error:**
```
could not read Username for 'https://github.com': terminal prompts disabled
The process '/usr/bin/git' failed with exit code 128
```

## Root Cause
The issue is caused by the `submodules: recursive` and `token: ${{ secrets.GH_PAT }}` configuration in the checkout step, which creates authentication conflicts when trying to access the `.cursor` submodule.

## Solution
- ✅ Remove `submodules: recursive` from checkout action
- ✅ Remove custom `token` parameter that's causing auth conflicts  
- ✅ Add manual submodule initialization with proper error handling
- ✅ Continue workflow execution even if submodules fail to initialize

## Changes Made
```yaml
# Before (causing auth failures)
- name: Checkout repository
  uses: actions/checkout@v4
  with:
    fetch-depth: 0
    submodules: recursive
    token: ${{ secrets.GH_PAT }}

# After (with manual handling)
- name: Checkout repository
  uses: actions/checkout@v4
  with:
    fetch-depth: 0

- name: Initialize submodules manually
  run: |
    # Initialize submodules with proper error handling
    if git submodule status | grep -q '^-'; then
      echo "Initializing submodules..."
      git submodule update --init --recursive || echo "Submodule initialization failed, continuing..."
    else
      echo "Submodules already initialized or not present"
    fi
```

## Testing
This approach:
- Avoids git authentication issues in CI environments
- Handles submodule failures gracefully without breaking the workflow
- Allows the autodoc process to continue even if `.cursor` submodule fails
